| `numbers.convolve_with()` | Every overlap (keep="all": n + m - 1 numbers), SciPy signal.convolve(mode="full"). | keep="same" (the middle n) or "overlap" (only full overlaps). | **P3.** The full convolution loses nothing; it is SciPy's and NumPy's default and R convolve(type = "open"). *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `numbers.change_time_step()` | Fourier resampling (SciPy signal.resample) to the number of readings that fills the same time. | test_method="polyphase" (resample_poly) or "filter then keep" (decimate). | **P5.** Fourier resampling keeps every frequency the new time step can show; it treats the recording as repeating, so the other methods are offered for recordings whose ends differ. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** A new step that would leave no readings is refused; a step that cannot be exact is said. |  |
| `numbers.change_time_step()` `test_method="cubic spline"` | SciPy cspline1d (no smoothing, ends mirrored) read at the new times by cspline1d_eval, from the first reading to the last. | test_method="quadratic spline", "fourier", "polyphase", "filter then keep". | **P3.** SciPy's defaults (lamb=0: the spline goes through every reading); no filter, so it is for making the step smaller or for smooth readings. *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `sdata.make_wave()` | Times from 0 in steps of time_step=; a square wave high for half of each cycle; SciPy signal's wave shapes. | shape="sine", "sawtooth", "chirp", "pulse", "impulse"; high_share=, rising_share=, sweep=. | **P3.** Half high is the usual square wave (SciPy's duty=0.5 default); starting at time 0 is SciPy's and R gsignal's convention. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** how_many= and frequency= must be above 0. |  |
| `sdata.make_wave()` `shape="maximum length sequence"` | SciPy max_len_seq with the fewest bits whose run is at least how_many= long, SciPy's taps and start. | how_many=. | **P3.** SciPy's own taps and start, so the run can be made again exactly. *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `sdata.make_wave()` `sweep="polynomial"` | SciPy sweep_poly with frequency= as the polynomial of the frequency in time, highest power first, phase 0. | sweep="linear" and the others with frequency=[start, end]. | **P3.** Highest power first, as NumPy poly1d and SciPy sweep_poly. *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `sdata.make_window_shape()` | The symmetric window (SciPy get_window(fftbins=False)), as R gsignal::hann and NumPy hanning. | periodic=True (the version used inside spectra, SciPy's own default). | **P3.** A window you look at or use as a filter is normally symmetric; the periodic one is for spectra, where SciPy uses it on its own. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** An unknown window name is refused with a list of common ones. |  |
| `sdata.system()` | The transfer function with the bottom's first coefficient scaled to 1, as SciPy's TransferFunction keeps it. | numerator=/denominator=, poles=/zeros=/gain=, a=/b=/c=/d=; time_step= for a system in steps. | **P3.** Scaling the first coefficient to 1 gives every system one way to be written, so the same system always prints the same. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** An improper formula (top of higher power than the bottom) is refused. |  |
| `system.step_response()` | 200 evenly spaced times from 0 to until= (SciPy chooses the end from the poles when until= is left out). | until=, how_many=, time_points=. | **P5.** 200 points draw a smooth curve for most systems; SciPy's own choice of end time follows the slowest pole. *Basis: starting value.* **Checks:** time_points= must start at 0 and be evenly spaced. |  |
| `system.impulse_response()` | 200 evenly spaced times from 0 to until=, as step_response. | until=, how_many=, time_points=. | **P5.** As system.step_response. *Basis: starting value.* **Checks:** As system.step_response. |  |
| `system.response_to()` | The system starts at rest and each input holds until the next time (zero order hold, SciPy lsim(interp=False)); the times from the step the inputs remember. | time_points=. | **P3.** A zero order hold is what a computer that reads and acts in steps does, and R control::lsim does the same; SciPy's own default joins the inputs by straight lines. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** Inputs whose time step differs from a system in steps are refused, with the line that fixes it. |  |
| `system.frequency_response()` | 200 frequencies evenly spaced on a log scale, in radians per second (cycles per unit of time for a filter); the phase unwrapped. | between=, how_many=, unit="cycles per second". | **P3.** Radians per second is the unit of R control, SciPy and MATLAB; an unwrapped phase shows the delay growing instead of jumping by 360 degrees. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** Frequencies above what a system in steps can show are refused. |  |
| `system.frequency_response()` `with_delay=` | SciPy group_delay in steps, times the time step. | with_delay=False (the default). | **P3.** SciPy's group delay, as R gsignal::grpdelay (the same numbers, in steps). *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** Only for systems in steps. |  |
| `system.poles()` | Each pole with its distance from 0, damping ratio and natural frequency, as R control::damp. | None. | **P3.** The damping ratio and natural frequency say how a pole moves the output; R control::damp's definitions are the textbook ones. *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `system.is_stable()` | Stable only when every pole lies strictly inside: a real part below 0 (inside the unit circle in steps). | None. | **P3.** A pole on the edge gives an output that never settles, so it does not count as stable (bounded-input, bounded-output stability). *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `system.steady_gain()` | The formula at s = 0 (z = 1 in steps), as R control::dcgain. | None. | **P3.** Where the output settles for a constant input of 1 is the formula at zero frequency; simplydata says when the output never settles there. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** A pole at s = 0 gives a gain without limit, and a system that is not stable is named. |  |
| `system.in_steps()` | Zero order hold (the input stays constant between steps), as R control::c2d. | test_method="bilinear", "forward difference", "backward difference". | **P3.** The zero order hold matches what a computer that acts in steps does, and it is R control's and MATLAB's c2d default. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** A system already in steps is refused. |  |
| `system.then()` | In series the formulas multiply, as R control::series. | add() for side by side, with_feedback() for a loop. | **P3.** That is what joining two systems in series means; there is no other choice. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** Systems of different kinds (continuous and in steps) are refused. |  |
| `system.add()` | Side by side the formulas add over a common bottom, as R control::parallel. | then() for in series. | **P3.** That is what joining two systems in parallel means. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** Systems of different kinds (continuous and in steps) are refused. |  |
| `system.with_feedback()` | Negative feedback through 1 (the output itself): G / (1 + G H) with H = 1, as R control::feedback. | through= another system; sign="positive". | **P3.** Negative unity feedback is the usual control loop and R control's default. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** Systems of different kinds are refused. |  |
| `system.table()` | The transfer function: one row per power, highest first, the top padded with zeros. | form="state space" (SciPy tf2ss, as R control::tf2ss) or "poles and zeros". | **P3.** Highest power first is how SciPy, R control and MATLAB write the coefficients; padding puts the top and bottom of each power on one row. *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `system.table()` `form="sections"` | The filter's own sections for sdata.frequency_filter; SciPy tf2sos for any other system. | form="transfer function", "state space", "poles and zeros", "partial fractions". | **P3.** SciPy's own sections are more exact than sections made again from the formula. *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `system.table()` `form="partial fractions"` | SciPy residue (residuez for a system in steps), its default tolerance for repeated poles. | sdata.system(residues=, poles=) adds them up again. | **P3.** SciPy's partial fractions, as R gsignal::residue. *Basis: convention.* |  |
| `system.chart()` | The Bode chart: gain in decibels and the unwrapped phase in degrees against frequency on a log scale, 200 frequencies SciPy picks from the poles and zeros, in two panels. | show="step", "impulse", "poles and zeros", "nyquist"; between=, until=, unit=. | **P3.** The Bode chart is the usual first look at a system, as R control::bode and MATLAB bode draw it. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** Options that do not go with the chosen show= are refused. |  |
| `system.move_poles_to()` | SciPy place_poles (Tits and Yang's method) on the controller form of SciPy tf2ss. | There is no other method here (SciPy's other method is for systems with one input too). | **P3.** SciPy's default method; R control::place gives other gains for the same request, with a warning, so it is not the reference. *Basis: convention.* **Checks:** The number of poles must match; complex poles must come in pairs. |  |
